# Concept A proof chain, readable edition

Generated by `python -I redesign/qp/A/proofs/render_readable_a.py` from out/graph.json and explain_a.py (2026-11-18 08:10 UTC). Do not edit; edit explain_a.py. Presentation only: no number or limit here is defined by this file.

## How to read this

This is a checking list for the aircraft. Each card below is one claim: a number the design depends on, written so you can redo it yourself with a calculator.
A claim is a number plus a limit. For example, 'the predicted mass is 1.466 kg and must be at most 1.467 kg'. Every card answers the same six questions, in the same order.

1. **What is being checked.** One sentence: what the number is and why the aircraft needs it to be inside a limit.
2. **The formula.** The rule that turns inputs into the result, with every symbol explained in words and units.
3. **The inputs.** Each input with its value, unit and where it came from, named as a document, not an id. Inputs marked ESTIMATE have not been measured or confirmed yet. Inputs marked ASSUMPTION are a deliberate guess the team has written down.
4. **The arithmetic.** The inputs put into the formula, step by step. Redo it on a calculator; you should get the same answer to the digits shown.
5. **The result and the margin.** The result next to its limit, and how much room is left. A positive margin is a pass. A margin of +5 % means the result has 5 % to spare.
6. **How to check it in real life.** The test or measurement that would confirm it on a real aircraft. Tests marked 'planned' have not been run: nothing is built yet.

### Status words

- **UNVERIFIED**: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
- **CHECKED**: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- **VERIFIED**: A physical test has been run and passed. No claim has this status yet: nothing is built.
- **PROPOSED**: A value with a written basis but no calculation yet.

### About the numbers

- Results are shown to 5 significant figures and inputs as stored (up to 6), so a calculator should land on the last digit shown give or take 1.
- 'Stored, not recomputed' means the number comes from a report written by a bigger analysis (finite-element run, simulation, CAD collision check). The card names that report and how to rerun it; this chain does not redo it.
- Margins use the project's rule: for a limit that is not zero the margin is the fraction of the limit left over; for a limit of zero (margin of safety, overlap counts) it is the signed distance to zero in the claim's own unit.

## Mass

Will the aircraft weigh what we say it weighs? Every other number (thrust margin, stall speed, loads) is scaled by this one.

#### Predicted take-off mass (CAD basic x growth + overrun)
`CL-A-MASS-PRED` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** Adds up what the aircraft will really weigh (CAD mass plus growth plus build overrun) and checks it is under the 1.467 kg design target, so the thrust, stall and energy numbers all use a mass we can defend.

**2. The formula.**

$$ m = m_{\mathrm{basic}}\,g_f + f_{\mathrm{over}}\,m_{\mathrm{gate}} $$

In words: m = basic x gf + over x gate

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `gate` | the mass gate: the heaviest the finished aircraft may be |
| `over` | build overrun: extra mass allowed for glue, wire and things not drawn, as a fraction of the gate |
| `gf` | growth factor: multiplier on the CAD mass for parts that are heavier in real life than drawn |
| `basic` | total mass of every part as drawn in the CAD model |
| `m` | predicted take-off mass |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `basic` — total mass of every part as drawn in the CAD model | 1.27024 | kg | [CAD mass and CG (sitl/cad_mass.json)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/sitl/cad_mass.json) (mass_basic_kg, cg_x_m, cg_y_m (regenerated by FIXES2, 2026-11-15)) | sourced |
| `gf` — growth factor: multiplier on the CAD mass for parts that are heavier in real life than drawn | 1.1174 |  | [Mass gate, concept A (MASS-GATE.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MASS-GATE.md) (s1 verdict, s4 overrun, s5 CG, s7 QP-4 FIXES2 update) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `over` — build overrun: extra mass allowed for glue, wire and things not drawn, as a fraction of the gate | 0.032 |  | [Mass gate, concept A (MASS-GATE.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MASS-GATE.md) (s1 verdict, s4 overrun, s5 CG, s7 QP-4 FIXES2 update) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `gate` — the mass gate: the heaviest the finished aircraft may be | 1.47 | kg | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- predicted take-off mass: `m = basic × gf + over × gate` = `1.27024 × 1.1174 + 0.032 × 1.47` = **1.4664 kg**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 1.4664 vs 1.4664.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **1.4664 kg**, limit ≤ 1.467 kg, margin **+0.04%**
- Limit comes from: A-D3 (D-207): design target <= 1.467 kg predicted incl. growth and overrun (D-195: 1.46; D-205: 1.465; the D-202 hub added 16.7 g basic, D-205 cut 10.7 g, the D-207 fillets and longer block added 1.3 g; 3 g left under the 1.47 kg gate, which is unchanged). 1.467 kg is the design target (D-207). It sits 3 g under the 1.47 kg mass gate so that a small overrun is noticed before the gate is hit.
- Passes. The result is 0.04 % under the limit (0.00059 kg to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - gf: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (growth factor: multiplier on the CAD mass for parts that are heavier in real life than drawn)
  - over: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (build overrun: extra mass allowed for glue, wire and things not drawn, as a fraction of the gate)
- Verifies: A-D3 Design target <= 1.467 kg; structure <= 494 g

**6. How to check it in real life.** Weigh-in test T-A-WEIGH: weigh the foam block, printed parts, epoxy joint and servo as they are built, then the whole aircraft with battery. As-built mass must be at most 1.47 kg. Use a kitchen scale good to 1 g.
- Planned test `T-A-WEIGH` (planned): Weigh-in: foam block, printed parts, epoxy joint, lead strip, one servo, then the aircraft. BOARD QP-4 s1 and FIXES2 s9: >= 27.2 g of the 41.6 g EST cuts must be real; as-built <= 1.47 kg (D-190; the +-5 % window holds to 1.478 kg)

#### Predicted mass, itemised growth by maturity (cross-check)
`CL-A-MASS-ITEM` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** A second, independent way to predict the mass: each item gets its own growth allowance based on how mature it is (weighed vendor part = 0 %, drawn printed part = 20 %). If the two methods disagree, one of them is wrong.

**2. The formula.**

$$ m = \frac{\sum_i m_i\,(1+\gamma_i) + f_{\mathrm{over}}\,m_{\mathrm{gate}}\cdot 1000}{1000} $$

In words: m = (sum over items of mass_i x (1 + growth_i) + over x gate x 1000) / 1000

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `items` | table of item mass (g) and growth fraction |
| `over` | build overrun: extra mass allowed for glue, wire and things not drawn, as a fraction of the gate |
| `gate` | the mass gate: the heaviest the finished aircraft may be |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `items` — table of item mass (g) and growth fraction | table, 12 rows (below) | g, fraction | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `over` — build overrun: extra mass allowed for glue, wire and things not drawn, as a fraction of the gate | 0.032 |  | [Mass gate, concept A (MASS-GATE.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MASS-GATE.md) (s1 verdict, s4 overrun, s5 CG, s7 QP-4 FIXES2 update) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `gate` — the mass gate: the heaviest the finished aircraft may be | 1.47 | kg | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |

Table `items`:

| row | item mass (g) | growth fraction |
|---|---|---|
| wing | 221.7 | 0.2 |
| fins + feet | 116.6 | 0.2 |
| motors | 260 | 0.05 |
| props | 44.2 | 0.05 |
| adapters + spinner nuts | 28.8 | 0.1 |
| ESC | 20.04 | 0.2 |
| pack | 270 | 0 |
| strap | 3 | 0.2 |
| avionics + harness | 101.18 | 0.2 |
| servos | 47.54 | 0.05 |
| cage | 119.2 | 0.2 |
| hardware | 26.08 | 0.2 |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- sum of item mass x (1 + growth): `sum_g = sum(r[0] × (1+r[1]) for r in items)` = **1400.4 g**
- predicted mass: `m = (sum_g + over × gate × 1000) / 1000` = `(1400.37 + 0.032 × 1.47 × 1000) / 1000` = **1.4474 kg**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 1.4474 vs 1.4474.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **1.4474 kg**, limit ≤ 1.467 kg, margin **+1.3%**
- Limit comes from: A-D3 (D-207): design target <= 1.467 kg predicted incl. growth and overrun (D-195: 1.46; D-205: 1.465; the D-202 hub added 16.7 g basic, D-205 cut 10.7 g, the D-207 fillets and longer block added 1.3 g; 3 g left under the 1.47 kg gate, which is unchanged). Same 1.467 kg design target as the first method.
- Passes. The result is 1.3 % under the limit (0.02 kg to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - items: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (table of item mass (g) and growth fraction)
  - over: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (build overrun: extra mass allowed for glue, wire and things not drawn, as a fraction of the gate)
- Verifies: A-D3 Design target <= 1.467 kg; structure <= 494 g

**6. How to check it in real life.** Same weigh-in as the first mass claim (T-A-WEIGH). Weighing each item separately also tells you which row of the table is off.

#### EST cuts that must be real for <= 1.467 kg
`CL-A-CUTS-REQUIRED` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The mass target only works if estimated weight cuts (lighter foam, lighter printed parts) are really achieved. This finds how many grams of those cuts must be real, out of the 41.6 g the team is counting on.

**2. The formula.**

$$ X = X_{\mathrm{cuts}} - \frac{m_{\mathrm{target}} - m}{g_f}\cdot 1000 $$

In words: X = cuts - (target - m) / gf x 1000

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `cuts` | grams of weight cuts the plan assumes (all estimates) |
| `target` | design target mass |
| `m` | predicted mass |
| `gf` | growth factor: multiplier on the CAD mass for parts that are heavier in real life than drawn |
| `X` | grams of cuts that must be real |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cuts` — grams of weight cuts the plan assumes (all estimates) | 41.6 | g | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `target` — design target mass | 1.467 | kg | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |
| `m` — predicted mass | 1.46641 | kg | Predicted take-off mass (CAD basic x growth + overrun) | result of an UNVERIFIED claim |
| `gf` — growth factor: multiplier on the CAD mass for parts that are heavier in real life than drawn | 1.1174 |  | [Mass gate, concept A (MASS-GATE.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MASS-GATE.md) (s1 verdict, s4 overrun, s5 CG, s7 QP-4 FIXES2 update) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- cuts that must be real: `x_g = cuts - (target - m) / gf × 1000` = `41.6 - (1.467 - 1.46641) / 1.1174 × 1000` = **41.069 g**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 41.069 vs 41.069.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **41.069 g**, limit ≤ 41.6 g, margin **+1.3%**
- Limit comes from: FIXES2 s5, s7: 41.6 g of EST cuts already in the CAD basic mass (foam 16.6, printed 18.0, hardware 7.0). X may not exceed the cuts available: if it did, even all the cuts would not reach the target.
- Passes. The result is 1.3 % under the limit (0.53 g to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - cuts: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (grams of weight cuts the plan assumes (all estimates))
  - m: result of an UNVERIFIED claim (predicted mass)
  - gf: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (growth factor: multiplier on the CAD mass for parts that are heavier in real life than drawn)
  - assumption: 41.6 g of EST cuts are real
- Verifies: A-D3 Design target <= 1.467 kg; structure <= 494 g

**6. How to check it in real life.** Weigh the foam block, the printed parts and the epoxy joint (T-A-WEIGH): at least 27.2 g of the 41.6 g must be real.
- Planned test `T-A-WEIGH` (planned): Weigh-in: foam block, printed parts, epoxy joint, lead strip, one servo, then the aircraft. BOARD QP-4 s1 and FIXES2 s9: >= 27.2 g of the 41.6 g EST cuts must be real; as-built <= 1.47 kg (D-190; the +-5 % window holds to 1.478 kg)

## Balance: centre of gravity and static margin

Does the aircraft balance in the right place, and will it fly straight (stay pitch-stable) in cruise?

#### CG aft of the leading edge, CAD as drawn
`CL-A-CG` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** The aircraft must balance at least 19 mm aft of the wing leading edge or it will be nose-heavy and the elevons cannot pitch it over in transition.

**2. The formula.**

$$ x_{CG} = \frac{\sum_i m_i\,x_i}{\sum_i m_i} $$

In words: CG = sum(m_i x x_i) / sum(m_i)  over every part

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `v` | CG position aft of the leading edge |

This number is a stored result, not recomputed in this chain. It comes from: the CAD model: every part's mass and centroid (sitl/cad_mass.json); CG = sum(mass x position) / sum(mass).

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

#### CG at the aft end of the pack trim travel
`CL-A-CG-TRIM-AFT` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** The battery can slide in the tray to correct the CG after the first weigh-in. This checks that, at the aft end of that slide, the CG is still inside the hard aft limit.

**2. The formula.**

$$ x_{CG,\mathrm{aft}} = x_{CG} + \frac{m_{\mathrm{pack}}\,\Delta x_{\mathrm{aft}}}{m_{\mathrm{basic}}} $$

In words: CG_aft = CG + m_pack x trim / m_basic

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `cg` | CG from CAD |
| `pack` | battery mass |
| `trim` | pack slide aft of the CAD position |
| `basic` | CAD mass |
| `trim_fwd` | pack slide forward of the CAD position |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cg` — CG from CAD | 21.1007 | mm | [CAD mass and CG (sitl/cad_mass.json)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/sitl/cad_mass.json) (mass_basic_kg, cg_x_m, cg_y_m (regenerated by FIXES2, 2026-11-15)) | sourced |
| `pack` — battery mass | 270 | g | [BOM-A.csv (single BOM, 38 lines)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/BOM-A.csv) (vendor price per line; date_checked column 2026-11-14 / 15 for verified lines) | sourced |
| `trim` — pack slide aft of the CAD position | 10 | mm | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | sourced |
| `trim_fwd` — pack slide forward of the CAD position | 6 | mm | [CAD mass and CG (sitl/cad_mass.json)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/sitl/cad_mass.json) (mass_basic_kg, cg_x_m, cg_y_m (regenerated by FIXES2, 2026-11-15)) | sourced |
| `basic` — CAD mass | 1.27024 | kg | [CAD mass and CG (sitl/cad_mass.json)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/sitl/cad_mass.json) (mass_basic_kg, cg_x_m, cg_y_m (regenerated by FIXES2, 2026-11-15)) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- CG shift per trim: `shift = pack × trim / (basic × 1000)` = `270 × 10 / (1.27024 × 1000)` = **2.1256 mm**
- CG with the pack at the aft end: `aft_mm = cg + shift` = `21.1007 + 2.12558` = **23.226 mm**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 23.226 vs 23.226.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **23.226 mm**, limit ≤ 28.5 mm, margin **+18.5%**
- Limit comes from: A-P4 hard aft limit. 28.5 mm is the hard aft CG limit: beyond it the static margin falls to the minimum.
- Passes. The result is 19 % under the limit (5.3 mm to spare).
- Status CHECKED: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- Verifies: A-P4 CG band, static margin, lateral CG

**6. How to check it in real life.** Move the real pack in the tray slots (T-A-CG-BREADBOARD) and measure the CG at each end.

#### Static margin at the CAD CG
`CL-A-SM` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** Static margin is the aircraft's built-in pitch stability. Below 5 % of the chord it gets twitchy and the autopilot has less margin.

**2. The formula.**

$$ \mathrm{SM} = \frac{x_{NP} - x_{CG}}{c}\times 100\ \% $$

In words: SM = (NP - CG) / chord x 100

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `np` | neutral point: how far aft of the leading edge the aircraft is neutrally stable (assumed) |
| `cg` | CG from CAD |
| `c` | wing chord |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `np` — neutral point: how far aft of the leading edge the aircraft is neutrally stable (assumed) | 40.7 | mm | Neutral point 40.7 mm aft of LE | ASSUMPTION: unverified estimate |
| `cg` — CG from CAD | 21.1007 | mm | [CAD mass and CG (sitl/cad_mass.json)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/sitl/cad_mass.json) (mass_basic_kg, cg_x_m, cg_y_m (regenerated by FIXES2, 2026-11-15)) | sourced |
| `c` — wing chord | 245 | mm | [Mass gate, concept A (MASS-GATE.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MASS-GATE.md) (s1 verdict, s4 overrun, s5 CG, s7 QP-4 FIXES2 update) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- static margin: `sm_pct = (np - cg) / c × 100` = `(40.7 - 21.1007) / 245 × 100` = **7.9997 % chord**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 7.9997 vs 7.9997.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **7.9997 % chord**, limit ≥ 5 % chord, margin **+60.0%**
- Limit comes from: A-P4. At least 5 % chord is the requirement A-P4.
- Passes. The result is 60 % above the limit (3 % chord to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - np: ASSUMPTION: unverified estimate (neutral point: how far aft of the leading edge the aircraft is neutrally stable (assumed))
  - assumption: Neutral point 40.7 mm aft of LE
- Verifies: A-P4 CG band, static margin, lateral CG

**6. How to check it in real life.** Measure NP and CG on the built aircraft (T-A-CG-BREADBOARD); the neutral point input is an assumption until then.
- Planned test `T-A-CG-BREADBOARD` (planned): CG breadboard on a knife edge (M5, M6). Measure CG and NP on the built aircraft; trim the pack +-10 mm in the tray slots; CG 20-26 mm, SM >= 5 %

## Hover thrust, motors and ESC

Can four motors lift it with margin, and do the motors and speed controllers stay inside their current ratings?

#### Hover T/W fresh at the 1.47 kg gate (0.90 cap)
`CL-A-TW-FRESH-GATE` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** With a full battery and the mass at the 1.47 kg gate, can the four motors lift at least 2.0 times the aircraft's weight at the 0.90 throttle cap?

**2. The formula.**

$$ \frac{T}{W} = \frac{4\,T_1\cos\theta\;k_{\mathrm{derate}}\,k_{\mathrm{hot}}}{m\,g} $$

In words: T/W = (4 x T x cos(cant) x derate x hot) / (m x g)  where T is the thrust of one APC 9x4.5MR propeller at the rpm that the MN2212 motor reaches on the duty-capped bus voltage. Entry window = (0.80 x T/W_sag - 1.33) / (0.80 x T/W_sag + 1.33).

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `m` | aircraft mass used |
| `g` | gravitational acceleration |
| `kv` | motor speed constant (rpm per volt) |
| `rm_ohm` | motor winding resistance |
| `i0_a` | motor no-load current |
| `rp_ohm` | battery pack internal resistance |
| `derate` | knock-down on the published thrust table |
| `cant_deg` | tilt of each thrust line |
| `download` | fraction of thrust left after the propeller wash hits the wing |
| `hot_sag` | extra knock-down on thrust for a hot, nearly empty pack |
| `spin_cap` | highest throttle the software allows (duty) |
| `v_fresh` | full pack voltage |
| `v_sag_rest` | worn pack voltage at rest |
| `apc_table` | vendor static thrust and power at each rpm |
| `lo` | lowest entry thrust the transition needs, in multiples of weight |
| `hi_frac` | highest entry thrust allowed, as a fraction of the sagged thrust |
| `duty` | throttle of the one motor |
| `T/W` | thrust-to-weight ratio |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `apc_table` — vendor static thrust and power at each rpm | table, 8 rows (below) | rpm, N, W | [prop_a.py (APC 9x4.5MR + MN2212 model)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/prop_a.py) (static rows of apcprop.com PER3_9x45MR.dat (read 2026-11-14 by the file's author), T-Motor MN2212 page Kv / Rm; I0, pack R and the 0.93 derate are estimates) | sourced |
| `kv` — motor speed constant (rpm per volt) | 780 | rpm/V | [prop_a.py (APC 9x4.5MR + MN2212 model)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/prop_a.py) (static rows of apcprop.com PER3_9x45MR.dat (read 2026-11-14 by the file's author), T-Motor MN2212 page Kv / Rm; I0, pack R and the 0.93 derate are estimates) | sourced |
| `rm_ohm` — motor winding resistance | 0.173 | ohm | [prop_a.py (APC 9x4.5MR + MN2212 model)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/prop_a.py) (static rows of apcprop.com PER3_9x45MR.dat (read 2026-11-14 by the file's author), T-Motor MN2212 page Kv / Rm; I0, pack R and the 0.93 derate are estimates) | sourced |
| `i0_a` — motor no-load current | 0.5 | A | [prop_a.py (APC 9x4.5MR + MN2212 model)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/prop_a.py) (static rows of apcprop.com PER3_9x45MR.dat (read 2026-11-14 by the file's author), T-Motor MN2212 page Kv / Rm; I0, pack R and the 0.93 derate are estimates) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

| `derate` — knock-down on the published thrust table | 0.93 |  | [prop_a.py (APC 9x4.5MR + MN2212 model)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/prop_a.py) (static rows of apcprop.com PER3_9x45MR.dat (read 2026-11-14 by the file's author), T-Motor MN2212 page Kv / Rm; I0, pack R and the 0.93 derate are estimates) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

#### Hover T/W sagged pack at the 1.47 kg gate (0.90 cap)
`CL-A-TW-SAG-GATE` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** With a nearly empty battery (voltage sagging) at the gate mass, can the motors still lift at least 1.6 times the weight? This is the worst state for hover and landing.

**2. The formula.**

$$ \frac{T}{W} = \frac{4\,T_1\cos\theta\;k_{\mathrm{derate}}\,k_{\mathrm{hot}}}{m\,g} $$

In words: T/W = (4 x T x cos(cant) x derate x hot) / (m x g)  where T is the thrust of one APC 9x4.5MR propeller at the rpm that the MN2212 motor reaches on the duty-capped bus voltage. Entry window = (0.80 x T/W_sag - 1.33) / (0.80 x T/W_sag + 1.33).

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|

| `kv` | motor speed constant (rpm per volt) |
| `rm_ohm` | motor winding resistance |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- T/W = total sagged thrust (gf) / weight (gf): `tw = sag_cap_gf / (m × 1000)` = `2716.67 / (1.47 × 1000)` = **1.8481 **

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 1.8481 vs 1.8481.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **1.8481 **, limit ≥ 1.6 , margin **+15.5%**
- Limit comes from: A-P1, A-P2 (worst pack state 20 % rest, 3.73 V/cell). 1.6 is the requirement A-P2 for the worst pack state.
- Passes. The result is 16 % above the limit (0.25  to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - i0_a: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (motor no-load current)
  - rp_ohm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (battery pack internal resistance)
  - derate: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (knock-down on the published thrust table)
  - hot_sag: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (extra knock-down on thrust for a hot, nearly empty pack)
- Verifies: A-P1 Mass gate 1.47 kg and T/W at the gate; A-P2 Hover T/W >= 2.0 fresh, >= 1.6 worst pack state

**6. How to check it in real life.** Thrust stand test T-A-BENCH-THRUST: one MN2212 with one APC 9x4.5MR on a 16.8 V and a 14.9 V supply, throttle to 0.90, read the load cell. Pass: fresh at least 780 gf and sag at least 624 gf per motor.
- Planned test `T-A-BENCH-THRUST` (planned): Bench thrust stand, one MN2212 + APC 9x4.5MR (M1). Order 1 kit, 16.8 V and 14.9 V supply, 0-100 % in steps: gf, A, rpm; pass fresh >= 780 gf and sag >= 624 gf per motor at the 0.90 cap (bench_analysis.py)

#### Hover T/W fresh at the predicted mass (0.90 cap)
`CL-A-TW-FRESH-PRED` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The same fresh-pack thrust-to-weight check at the predicted mass instead of the gate mass.

**2. The formula.**

$$ \frac{T}{W} = \frac{4\,T_1\cos\theta\;k_{\mathrm{derate}}\,k_{\mathrm{hot}}}{m\,g} $$

In words: T/W = (4 x T x cos(cant) x derate x hot) / (m x g)  where T is the thrust of one APC 9x4.5MR propeller at the rpm that the MN2212 motor reaches on the duty-capped bus voltage. Entry window = (0.80 x T/W_sag - 1.33) / (0.80 x T/W_sag + 1.33).

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `m` | aircraft mass used |
| `g` | gravitational acceleration |
| `kv` | motor speed constant (rpm per volt) |
| `rm_ohm` | motor winding resistance |
| `i0_a` | motor no-load current |
| `rp_ohm` | battery pack internal resistance |
| `derate` | knock-down on the published thrust table |
| `cant_deg` | tilt of each thrust line |
| `download` | fraction of thrust left after the propeller wash hits the wing |
| `hot_sag` | extra knock-down on thrust for a hot, nearly empty pack |
| `spin_cap` | highest throttle the software allows (duty) |
| `v_fresh` | full pack voltage |
| `v_sag_rest` | worn pack voltage at rest |
| `apc_table` | vendor static thrust and power at each rpm |
| `lo` | lowest entry thrust the transition needs, in multiples of weight |
| `hi_frac` | highest entry thrust allowed, as a fraction of the sagged thrust |
| `duty` | throttle of the one motor |
| `T/W` | thrust-to-weight ratio |

| 11000 | 13.149 | 205.68 |
| 12000 | 15.706 | 266.981 |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- The motor speed is found by balancing motor torque against propeller torque (prop_a.py, bisection over the vendor table above); that iteration is not hand-friendly, so this card shows the last step by calculator and gives the command to rerun the whole model: python -I redesign/qp/A/prop_a.py   (prints the same thrusts in gf)

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **1.8526 **, limit ≥ 1.6 , margin **+15.8%**
- Limit comes from: A-P1, A-P2 (worst pack state 20 % rest, 3.73 V/cell). 1.6 is the requirement A-P2.
- Passes. The result is 16 % above the limit (0.25  to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - i0_a: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (motor no-load current)
  - rp_ohm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (battery pack internal resistance)
  - derate: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (knock-down on the published thrust table)
  - hot_sag: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (extra knock-down on thrust for a hot, nearly empty pack)
  - m: result of an UNVERIFIED claim (aircraft mass used)
- Verifies: A-P2 Hover T/W >= 2.0 fresh, >= 1.6 worst pack state

**6. How to check it in real life.** Thrust stand test T-A-BENCH-THRUST: one MN2212 with one APC 9x4.5MR on a 16.8 V and a 14.9 V supply, throttle to 0.90, read the load cell. Pass: fresh at least 780 gf and sag at least 624 gf per motor.

#### Transition entry window at the predicted mass (0.90 cap)
`CL-A-WINDOW-PRED` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** At the start of the transition from hover to cruise the aircraft needs a minimum entry thrust and has a maximum it can safely reach. The window between them must be at least 5 % wide on each side; this is the predicted-mass case.

**2. The formula.**

$$ w = \frac{0.80\,(T/W)_{\mathrm{sag}} - 1.33}{0.80\,(T/W)_{\mathrm{sag}} + 1.33}\times 100\ \% $$

In words: window = (0.80 x TW_sag - 1.33) / (0.80 x TW_sag + 1.33) x 100

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `m` | aircraft mass used |
| `g` | gravitational acceleration |
| `kv` | motor speed constant (rpm per volt) |
| `rm_ohm` | motor winding resistance |
| `i0_a` | motor no-load current |
| `rp_ohm` | battery pack internal resistance |
| `derate` | knock-down on the published thrust table |
| `cant_deg` | tilt of each thrust line |
| `download` | fraction of thrust left after the propeller wash hits the wing |
| `hot_sag` | extra knock-down on thrust for a hot, nearly empty pack |
| `spin_cap` | highest throttle the software allows (duty) |
| `v_fresh` | full pack voltage |
| `v_sag_rest` | worn pack voltage at rest |
| `apc_table` | vendor static thrust and power at each rpm |
| `lo` | lowest entry thrust the transition needs, in multiples of weight |
| `hi_frac` | highest entry thrust allowed, as a fraction of the sagged thrust |
| `duty` | throttle of the one motor |
| `window` | half-width of the usable entry-thrust window |

- window: `w = (hi_frac × sag_cap - lo) / (hi_frac × sag_cap + lo) × 100` = `(0.8 × 1.85261 - 1.33) / (0.8 × 1.85261 + 1.33) × 100` = **5.4083 %**

- Passes. The result is 8.2 % above the limit (0.41 % to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.

- Verifies: A-P3 Transition window >= +-5 %

**6. How to check it in real life.** C3 time-domain pitch-over run at the as-weighed mass (T-A-C3-PITCHOVER), and the thrust stand (T-A-BENCH-THRUST).
- Planned test `T-A-BENCH-THRUST` (planned): Bench thrust stand, one MN2212 + APC 9x4.5MR (M1). Order 1 kit, 16.8 V and 14.9 V supply, 0-100 % in steps: gf, A, rpm; pass fresh >= 780 gf and sag >= 624 gf per motor at the 0.90 cap (bench_analysis.py)
- Planned test `T-A-C3-PITCHOVER` (planned): C3 time-domain pitch-over run at the as-weighed mass. MASS-GATE s1: above 1.486 kg the aircraft flies only if the run shows >= +-5 % at the as-weighed mass

#### Largest mass with a +-5 % window (0.90 cap)

#### One motor at the 0.90 spin cap, winding current
`CL-A-MOTOR-I` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** A single motor can be asked for 0.90 throttle while the other three hold hover. This checks that its winding current stays under the motor's 13 A rating so it does not overheat.

**2. The formula.**

`I = phase current of one motor at duty 0.90 (prop_a.py one_motor), three motors at hover, 1.50 kg, fresh pack`

| `hi_frac` | highest entry thrust allowed, as a fraction of the sagged thrust |

| `i_a` | winding current of the loaded motor |

#### ESC channel and connector current required (1.25 x uncapped peak)
`CL-A-ESC-I` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** ESCs and connectors must handle more than the worst current, with a 25 % cushion. This finds the current they must be rated for.

**2. The formula.**

$$ I_{\mathrm{req}} = 1.25\; I_{\mathrm{peak}} $$

In words: I_required = 1.25 x peak current of one motor with no throttle cap (full duty, three motors at hover, 1.50 kg, fresh pack)

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `m` | aircraft mass used |
| `g` | gravitational acceleration |
| `kv` | motor speed constant (rpm per volt) |
| `rm_ohm` | motor winding resistance |
| `i0_a` | motor no-load current |
| `rp_ohm` | battery pack internal resistance |
| `derate` | knock-down on the published thrust table |
| `cant_deg` | tilt of each thrust line |
| `download` | fraction of thrust left after the propeller wash hits the wing |
| `hot_sag` | extra knock-down on thrust for a hot, nearly empty pack |
| `spin_cap` | highest throttle the software allows (duty) |
| `v_fresh` | full pack voltage |
| `v_sag_rest` | worn pack voltage at rest |
| `apc_table` | vendor static thrust and power at each rpm |
| `lo` | lowest entry thrust the transition needs, in multiples of weight |
| `hi_frac` | highest entry thrust allowed, as a fraction of the sagged thrust |
| `duty` | throttle of the one motor |
| `factor` | safety factor on current |
| `i_a` | current the ESC channel and connector must carry |

## Structures and loads

Do the tubes, joints, printed parts and foam survive the loads we expect, with the safety factor on top?

#### Static tip-over angle on the fin tips
`CL-A-TIPOVER` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** Standing on its fin tips, the aircraft must not tip over if the ground slopes or a gust leans it. This is the tilt at which it starts to tip.

**2. The formula.**

`tip-over angle = arctan( half the foot spacing / CG height )`

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `v` | static tip-over angle |

This number is a stored result, not recomputed in this chain. It comes from: geometry in SIZING-A s4: feet 0.36 m apart, CG 0.275 m above the ground.

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v` — static tip-over angle | 33.2 | deg | [SIZING-A.md (QP-1 sizing of concept A)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-1/SIZING-A.md) (s4 fin-tip landing: 33.2 deg static tip angle) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- This value is read from the stored report named above; nothing to substitute.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **33.2 deg**, limit ≥ 30 deg, margin **+10.7%**
- Limit comes from: A-P9. At least 30 degrees (requirement A-P9).
- Passes. The result is 11 % above the limit (3.2 deg to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - v: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (static tip-over angle)
- Verifies: A-P9 Fin-tip landing tip-over >= 30 deg

**6. How to check it in real life.** Tilt the built aircraft on a board until it tips, read the board angle on a phone.

#### Spar 12x10 root bending, MS at 1.5 x n 3.4
`CL-A-MS-SPAR` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** The carbon spar is the beam that carries the lift of the whole wing to the fuselage. This checks it does not break at the highest expected load (3.4 g).

**2. The formula.**

$$ \mathrm{MS} = \frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{allow}}}{\sigma_{\mathrm{design}}} - 1 $$

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v`  | 2.73 |  | [Structures iteration 2 margins (iter2_margins.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/iter2_margins.md) (MS table at 1.5 x limit) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- Load: root bending moment 4.78 N m (unrelieved), 3.4 g limit x 1.725.
- MS = allowable / stress - 1 = 350 / 93.9 - 1 = **2.727** (stored 2.73; agrees to the printed rounding).
- Stress and allowable (MPa) are copied from the cited structures file, not part of the strict chain.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **2.73 **, limit ≥ 0 , margin **+2.73**
- Limit comes from: A-P13: MS >= 0 at 1.5 x limit.
- MS = +2.73 means the part can carry 3.73 × the design load before reaching its allowable, so it is using 27 % of what it can carry (utilisation 0.27). The design load already includes the factor of safety.
- Status CHECKED: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- Verifies: A-P13 Loads and flutter (G8)

**6. How to check it in real life.** Break a small test piece (coupon) on a test stand and use the measured strength instead of the estimated allowable; or load the built part with weights to the design load and check nothing yields. For the spar a three-point bend test of an off-cut tube is the cheapest check.

$$ \mathrm{MS} = \frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{allow}}}{\sigma_{\mathrm{design}}} - 1 $$

#### Bridge 16x14 torsion shear, F2 motor fault at the CFRP factor (bridge alone)
`CL-A-BRIDGE-TORSION-F2` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** If one motor goes to full power (an ESC cap failure) the hub tries to roll about the bridge tube. This checks the 16 x 14 carbon bridge tube does not fail in twist with the carbon design factor.

**2. The formula.**

$$ \tau = \frac{k\,M\,(d_o/2)}{J},\qquad J = \frac{\pi}{32}\left(d_o^{4} - d_i^{4}\right) $$

In words: tau = k x M x (OD / 2) / J,  J = pi / 32 x (OD^4 - ID^4)

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `od` | outer diameter of the bridge tube |
| `idd` | inner diameter |
| `m` | roll moment the tube must carry |
| `k` | factor on the load (1.725 carbon design factor, or 1.0 for a damage case) |
| `tau` | shear stress in the tube wall |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `od` — outer diameter of the bridge tube | 16 | mm | [Bridge tube torsion sizing, D-206 (bridge_torsion_d206.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/bridge_torsion_d206.md) (roll moments from the hub FEA load set; tau = M r / J; strut share bounds) | sourced |
| `idd` — inner diameter | 14 | mm | [Bridge tube torsion sizing, D-206 (bridge_torsion_d206.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/bridge_torsion_d206.md) (roll moments from the hub FEA load set; tau = M r / J; strut share bounds) | sourced |
| `m` — roll moment the tube must carry | 4.37 | N m | [Bridge tube torsion sizing, D-206 (bridge_torsion_d206.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/bridge_torsion_d206.md) (roll moments from the hub FEA load set; tau = M r / J; strut share bounds) | sourced |
| `k` — factor on the load (1.725 carbon design factor, or 1.0 for a damage case) | 1.725 |  | [Bridge tube torsion sizing, D-206 (bridge_torsion_d206.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/bridge_torsion_d206.md) (roll moments from the hub FEA load set; tau = M r / J; strut share bounds) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- J, torsion constant of the tube: `j = 3.141592653589793 / 32 × (od^4 - idd^4)` = `3.141592653589793 / 32 × (16^4 - 14^4)` = **2662.5 mm^4**
- shear stress in the tube: `tau_mpa = k × m × 1000 × (od / 2) / j` = `1.725 × 4.37 × 1000 × (16 / 2) / 2662.5` = **22.65 MPa**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 22.65 vs 22.65.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **22.65 MPa**, limit ≤ 30 MPa, margin **+24.5%**
- Limit comes from: fea_arm.py ALLOW cf_shear (EST, no coupon). 30 MPa is the estimated carbon shear allowable (no coupon test yet).
- Passes. The result is 24 % under the limit (7.3 MPa to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
- Verifies: A-P13 Loads and flutter (G8)

**6. How to check it in real life.** Twist a spare 16 x 14 tube on a lever arm with a known weight; the 30 MPa allowable is an estimate.

#### Foam shear around the spar, bond over |y| <= 0.20 m (FIXES2 rule), MS at 103 kPa basis
`CL-A-FOAM-SPAR-BOND` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The foam core is glued to the spar. In flight the lift load passes through that glue; this checks the foam shear around the spar.

**2. The formula.**

$$ \mathrm{MS} = \frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{allow}}}{\sigma_{\mathrm{design}}} - 1 $$

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v`  | 0.48 |  | [Foam checks at 103 kPa (foam_103.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/foam_103.md) (MS at the 103 kPa basis (compressive 103 V data sheet; tension 129, shear 86 EST)) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- Load: bond zone |y| <= 0.20 m.
- MS = allowable / stress - 1 = 86 / 58 - 1 = **0.483** (stored 0.48; agrees to the printed rounding).
- Stress and allowable (kPa) are copied from the cited structures file, not part of the strict chain.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **0.48 **, limit ≥ 0 , margin **+0.48**
- Limit comes from: A-P13: MS >= 0 at 1.5 x limit.
- MS = +0.48 means the part can carry 1.48 × the design load before reaching its allowable, so it is using 68 % of what it can carry (utilisation 0.68). The design load already includes the factor of safety.
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - v: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed
  - assumption: Foam tension 129 kPa and shear 86 kPa
- Verifies: A-P13 Loads and flutter (G8)

**6. How to check it in real life.** Cut a small block of the real foam (FOAMULAR 150) and load it in compression, shear and tension with weights or on a test stand; the shear and tension allowables are estimates.

## Cost

Does everything we buy fit inside the $2,000 limit, with room for prices that turn out higher?

#### BOM total incl. tax, shipping, FAA and crash reserve
`CL-A-COST-TOTAL` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** Adds up everything we must buy (parts, tax, shipping, FAA registration and a reserve for crash damage) and checks it is under $1,800, leaving a $200 cushion under the $2,000 cap.

**2. The formula.**

$$ C = G\,(1+t) + S + F + R $$

In words: total = goods x (1 + tax) + shipping + FAA + crash reserve

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `rows` | BOM lines: id, group, vendor, quantity, unit price (USD), unverified flag |
| `tax` | sales tax on goods |
| `faa` | FAA registration fee |
| `ship` | shipping per vendor |
| `bump` | price uplift for unverified lines (30 %) |
| `tubes` | BOM ids counted in the crash reserve |
| `budget` | the budget cap |

#### Margin to $2,000 if the unverified lines run 30 % over
`CL-A-COST-MARGIN30` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**4. The arithmetic.**

- goods on the unverified lines: `unv = sum(r[3] × r[4] for r in rows if r[5])` = **282.29 USD**
- uplift of those lines at +30 %, with tax (the crash reserve also rises a little): `extra = unv × bump × (1+tax)` = `282.29 × 0.3 × (1+0.1025)` = **93.367 USD**
- total with the uplift (the BOM calculation's own output): `t30 = total_30_usd` = `1899.95` = **1900 USD**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 1900 vs 1900.

- Limit comes from: CLAUDE.md: $2,000 total physical budget. $2,000 is the user's hard total (CLAUDE.md).

## Mission, energy and range

Does the battery last long enough for the mission, and does the aircraft stay inside the flying field?

#### Cruise time the mission needs (300 m reference lap)
`CL-A-CRUISE-NEED` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** How long must the aircraft cruise to fly the required racetrack laps, including a go-around and run-in? This is the time the battery must support.

**2. The formula.**

$$ R=\frac{V^{2}}{g\tan\phi},\quad t_{\mathrm{lap}}=\frac{2L+2\pi R}{V},\quad t_{\mathrm{need}} = t_{\mathrm{settle}} + (n_{\mathrm{laps}}+n_{\mathrm{ga}}+n_{\mathrm{run}})\,t_{\mathrm{lap}} $$

In words: R = V^2 / (g x tan(bank));  lap time = (2 x L_ref + 2 x pi x R) / V;  need = settle + (laps + go-around + run-in) x lap time

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `v` | cruise speed |
| `g` | gravity |
| `bank` | bank angle in turns |
| `leg` | straight leg length |
| `settle` | time to settle in cruise |
| `laps` | required laps |
| `ga` | go-around laps |
| `runin` | run-in laps |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v` — cruise speed | 17 | m/s | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | sourced |
| `g` — gravity | 9.807 | m/s2 | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `bank` — bank angle in turns | 30 | deg | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | sourced |
| `leg` — straight leg length | 300 | m | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `settle` — time to settle in cruise | 15 | s | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `laps` — required laps | 2 |  | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | sourced |
| `ga` — go-around laps | 1 |  | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | sourced |
| `runin` — run-in laps | 0.5 |  | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- turn radius: `radius = v^2 / (g × tan(bank°))` = `17^2 / (9.807 × tan(30°))` = **51.041 m**
- time for one lap: `lap_s = (2 × leg + 2 × π × radius) / v` = `(2 × 300 + 2 × π × 51.0414) / 17` = **54.159 s**
- cruise time needed: `need = settle + (laps + ga + runin) × lap_s` = `15 + (2 + 1 + 0.5) × 54.159` = **204.56 s**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 204.56 vs 204.56.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **204.56 s**, limit ≤ 210 s, margin **+2.6%**
- Limit comes from: fixed in the claim. At most 210 s (3.5 min, requirement A-D2).
- Passes. The result is 2.6 % under the limit (5.4 s to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - settle: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (time to settle in cruise)
- Verifies: A-D2 Mission: 3.0 min hover, 3.41 min cruise at 17 m/s

**6. How to check it in real life.** Fly the racetrack on a tethered or open-field test and time the cruise portion on the flight log.

#### Turn radius at 17 m/s, 30 deg bank
`CL-A-CRUISE-RADIUS` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The turn radius at cruise speed and 30 degrees of bank. It sets the size of the racetrack and so the fence distance.

**2. The formula.**

$$ R = \frac{V^{2}}{g\tan\phi} $$

In words: R = V^2 / (g x tan(bank))

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `v` | cruise speed |
| `g` | gravity |
| `bank` | bank angle in turns |

#### Cruise time to BATT_LOW at the predicted mass
`CL-A-CRUISE-AVAIL` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** How long can the battery actually power cruise, after hover, transition and climb take their share? Must exceed the time the mission needs.

**2. The formula.**

$$ t_{\mathrm{cruise}} = \frac{E_{\mathrm{usable}} - E_{\mathrm{hover}} - E_{\mathrm{trans}} - E_{\mathrm{climb}}}{P_{\mathrm{cruise}}}\times 3600 $$

In words: t = (E_usable - E_hover - E_transition - E_climb) / P_cruise x 3600

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `pack_ah` | pack capacity |
| `pack_v` | pack nominal voltage |
| `floor_frac` | usable fraction of capacity (down to the BATT_LOW floor) |
| `reserve_frac` | fraction kept as reserve |
| `hover_s` | hover time |
| `trans_wh_ref` | transition energy at reference mass |
| `climb_wh_ref` | climb energy at reference mass |
| `cruise_w_ref` | cruise power at reference mass |
| `ref_mass_kg` | reference mass for those three |
| `trans_exp` | how transition energy grows with mass |
| `cruise_exp` | how cruise power grows with mass |
| `m` | predicted mass |

| `reserve_frac` — fraction kept as reserve | 0.8 |  | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `hover_s` — hover time | 180 | s | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `trans_wh_ref` — transition energy at reference mass | 1.56 | Wh | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `climb_wh_ref` — climb energy at reference mass | 0.29 | Wh | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `cruise_w_ref` — cruise power at reference mass | 161 | W | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `ref_mass_kg` — reference mass for those three | 1.37 | kg | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `trans_exp` — how transition energy grows with mass | 1.5 |  | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `cruise_exp` — how cruise power grows with mass | 0.58 |  | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

Table `apc_table`:

| c1 | c2 | c3 |
|---|---|---|
| 5000 | 2.674 | 20.063 |
| 6000 | 3.859 | 34.224 |
| 7000 | 5.264 | 53.842 |
| 8000 | 6.892 | 79.836 |
| 9000 | 8.746 | 113.149 |
| 10000 | 10.83 | 154.758 |
| 11000 | 13.149 | 205.68 |
| 12000 | 15.706 | 266.981 |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- usable battery energy: `usable = pack_ah × pack_v × floor_frac × reserve_frac` = `2.3 × 14.8 × 0.9 × 0.8` = **24.509 Wh**
- energy for hover (hover power from the prop model): `hover_wh = hover_w × hover_s / 3600` = `212.987 × 180 / 3600` = **10.649 Wh**
- transition energy: `trans = trans_wh_ref × (m / ref_mass_kg)^trans_exp` = `1.56 × (1.46641 / 1.37)^1.5` = **1.7275 Wh**
- climb energy: `climb = climb_wh_ref × (m / ref_mass_kg)` = `0.29 × (1.46641 / 1.37)` = **0.31041 Wh**
- cruise power: `cruise_w2 = cruise_w_ref × (m / ref_mass_kg)^cruise_exp` = `161 × (1.46641 / 1.37)^0.58` = **167.48 W**
- cruise time available: `cruise_s = (usable - hover_wh - trans - climb) / cruise_w2 × 3600` = `(24.5088 - 10.6494 - 1.72753 - 0.310407) / 167.477 × 3600` = **254.11 s**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 254.11 vs 254.11.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **254.11 s**, limit ≥ 204.56 s, margin **+24.2%**
- Limit comes from: Cruise time the mission needs (300 m reference lap). At least the time the mission needs (previous claim).
- Passes. The result is 24 % above the limit (50 s to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - i0_a: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed
  - derate: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed
  - esc_eff: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed
  - ctrl_margin: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed
  - m: result of an UNVERIFIED claim (predicted mass)
  - trans_wh_ref: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (transition energy at reference mass)
  - climb_wh_ref: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (climb energy at reference mass)
  - cruise_w_ref: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (cruise power at reference mass)
- Verifies: A-D2 Mission: 3.0 min hover, 3.41 min cruise at 17 m/s

**6. How to check it in real life.** Fly a full mission profile on a bench or tether with a power meter (watt-hours used) and compare with the model.

#### Any-direction bound after a cruise BATT_LOW
`CL-A-FENCE` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** After the battery warning fires in cruise the aircraft keeps travelling for a while before it lands. This checks it stays inside the 240 m flying fence in any direction.

**2. The formula.**

$$ d = \frac{L}{2} + R + d_{\mathrm{run}} $$

In words: bound = leg / 2 + R + run-out

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `leg` | racetrack leg length |
| `r` | turn radius |
| `runout` | distance travelled after the warning |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `leg` — racetrack leg length | 150 | m | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `r` — turn radius | 51.0414 | m | Turn radius at 17 m/s, 30 deg bank | result of an UNVERIFIED claim |
| `runout` — distance travelled after the warning | 94 | m | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- worst-case distance from the field centre: `bound_m = leg / 2 + r + runout` = `150 / 2 + 51.0414 + 94` = **220.04 m**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 220.04 vs 220.04.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **220.04 m**, limit ≤ 240 m, margin **+8.3%**
- Limit comes from: A-P24: fence 250 m (240 m after FENCE_MARGIN 10). 240 m is the fence radius (requirement A-P24).
- Passes. The result is 8.3 % under the limit (20 m to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - r: result of an UNVERIFIED claim (turn radius)
  - runout: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (distance travelled after the warning)
- Verifies: A-P24 Fence and run-out

**6. How to check it in real life.** Set the geofence at 240 m in the autopilot and simulate a BATT_LOW trigger at each point of the racetrack.

## Flying qualities: stall, yaw, wind, control

Can it be steered and held steady in hover, in wind, and in fast flight?

#### 1.3 x clean stall speed at the 1.47 kg gate
`CL-A-STALL` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The slowest speed at which the wing still lifts the aircraft. Cruise must be at least 1.3 times this, or a small gust drops the wing.

**2. The formula.**

$$ V_s = \sqrt{\frac{2\,m\,g}{\rho\,S\,C_{L\max}}},\qquad \text{claim} = 1.3\,V_s $$

In words: Vs = sqrt(2 x m x g / (rho x S x CLmax));  claim = 1.3 x Vs

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `m` | gate mass |
| `g` | gravity |
| `rho` | air density |
| `s` | wing area |
| `cl` | wing's peak lift coefficient (an assumption) |
| `k` | safety ratio of cruise to stall |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m` — gate mass | 1.47 | kg | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |
| `g` — gravity | 9.807 | m/s2 | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `rho` — air density | 1.2 | kg/m3 | [Mission definition, concept A](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/MISSION.md) (s1 speeds, s2 racetrack) | sourced |
| `s` — wing area | 0.22 | m2 | [mission_gate_a.py (mission and mass-gate arithmetic)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/mission_gate_a.py) (items list, stall, T/W, energy and cruise-to-BATT_LOW lines) | sourced |
| `cl` — wing's peak lift coefficient (an assumption) | 0.75 |  | Clean CLmax 0.75 | ASSUMPTION: unverified estimate |
| `k` — safety ratio of cruise to stall | 1.3 |  | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- stall speed: `vs = (2 × m × g / (rho × s × cl))^0.5` = `(2 × 1.47 × 9.807 / (1.2 × 0.22 × 0.75))^0.5` = **12.067 m/s**
- 1.3 x stall speed: `k_vs = k × vs` = `1.3 × 12.0673` = **15.687 m/s**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 15.687 vs 15.687.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **15.687 m/s**, limit ≤ 17 m/s, margin **+7.7%**
- Limit comes from: MISSION s1: cruise 17 m/s IAS. At most the 17 m/s cruise speed (requirement A-P6).
- Passes. The result is 7.7 % under the limit (1.3 m/s to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - cl: ASSUMPTION: unverified estimate (wing's peak lift coefficient (an assumption))
  - assumption: Clean CLmax 0.75
- Verifies: A-P6 Stall: 1.3 Vs below cruise

**6. How to check it in real life.** First-cruise test (T-A-FIRST-CRUISE): slow the aircraft until it stalls at altitude and read airspeed; the measured stall replaces the CLmax assumption.
- Planned test `T-A-FIRST-CRUISE` (planned): First cruise: roll rate and measured stall (M8 extended). Measure the peak roll rate (>= 90 deg/s) and the clean stall; the measured CLmax replaces 0.75

#### Hover yaw authority (model) at +-0.10 throttle
`CL-A-YAW-MODEL` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** In hover the aircraft steers in yaw by slightly speeding two motors and slowing two. This checks it can make at least 0.35 N m of twist.

**2. The formula.**

$$ N = N_{\mathrm{ref}}\,k_{\mathrm{arm}}\,\frac{\sin\theta}{\sin\theta_{\mathrm{ref}}} + k_Q\,Q $$

In words: authority = cant_term x arm_factor x sin(cant) / sin(cant_ref) + q_gain x Q

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `cant_term_ref_nm` | yaw authority from cant at the reference cant angle |
| `arm_factor` | factor for the longer arm design |
| `cant_deg` | actual cant |
| `cant_ref_deg` | reference cant |
| `q_model_nmm` | rotor reaction torque from the model |
| `q_gain` | arm-factor scaling on Q |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cant_term_ref_nm` — yaw authority from cant at the reference cant angle | 0.201 | N m | [bench_analysis.py (yaw authority and bench limits)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/bench/bench_analysis.py) (CANT_TERM, AUTH_PASS 0.35 / AUTH_FAIL 0.30, Q_MIN 55 N mm) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `arm_factor` — factor for the longer arm design | 1.2 |  | [bench_analysis.py (yaw authority and bench limits)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/bench/bench_analysis.py) (CANT_TERM, AUTH_PASS 0.35 / AUTH_FAIL 0.30, Q_MIN 55 N mm) | sourced |
| `cant_deg` — actual cant | 17 | deg | [bench_analysis.py (yaw authority and bench limits)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/bench/bench_analysis.py) (CANT_TERM, AUTH_PASS 0.35 / AUTH_FAIL 0.30, Q_MIN 55 N mm) | sourced |
| `cant_ref_deg` — reference cant | 14 | deg | [bench_analysis.py (yaw authority and bench limits)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/bench/bench_analysis.py) (CANT_TERM, AUTH_PASS 0.35 / AUTH_FAIL 0.30, Q_MIN 55 N mm) | sourced |
| `q_model_nmm` — rotor reaction torque from the model | 59.7 | N mm | [D-184 yaw levers (D184.md)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-2/D184.md) (model Q 59.7 N mm gives 0.346 N m authority (marginally under 0.35)) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `q_gain` — arm-factor scaling on Q | 1.2 |  | [bench_analysis.py (yaw authority and bench limits)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/bench/bench_analysis.py) (CANT_TERM, AUTH_PASS 0.35 / AUTH_FAIL 0.30, Q_MIN 55 N mm) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- yaw authority: `n_m = cant_term_ref_nm × arm_factor × sin(cant_deg°) / sin(cant_ref_deg°) + q_gain × q_model_nmm / 1000` = `0.201 × 1.2 × sin(17°) / sin(14°) + 1.2 × 59.7 / 1000` = **0.36314 N m**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 0.36314 vs 0.36314.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **0.36314 N m**, limit ≥ 0.35 N m, margin **+3.8%**
- Limit comes from: A-P7. 0.35 N m is the pass level (requirement A-P7).
- Passes. The result is 3.8 % above the limit (0.013 N m to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - cant_term_ref_nm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (yaw authority from cant at the reference cant angle)
  - q_model_nmm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (rotor reaction torque from the model)
- Verifies: A-P7 Hover yaw authority >= 0.35 N m

**6. How to check it in real life.** Hanging yaw test (T-A-BENCH-YAW): the built aircraft on a pivot, apply +-0.10 throttle and measure the twisting force on a scale.
- Planned test `T-A-BENCH-YAW` (planned): Hanging yaw test, arm x1.2, cant 16 deg (M3). Hanging yaw plate at +-0.10 throttle: authority >= 0.35 N m go; 0.30-0.35 go with the 4 m/s card; < 0.30 R2 (BOARD G3)

#### Elevon use in transition (share of the 20 deg stop)
`CL-A-PITCH-USE` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** During the pitch-over transition the elevons deflect part of their travel. This checks they use at most 60 % of the 20 degree stop, leaving travel for gusts.

**2. The formula.**

$$ u = \frac{\delta_{\mathrm{peak}}}{\delta_{\mathrm{stop}}} $$

In words: use = peak elevon deflection / stop

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `peak` | largest elevon deflection in the transition |
| `stop` | mechanical stop |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `peak` — largest elevon deflection in the transition | 11.5 | deg | [QP-5 SITL runs R5_* (sitl/results_fwd.jsonl, runs/R5_*)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/sitl/jobs_qp5.txt) (cant 17, D-191 gains, CAD mass / CG / inertia 2026-11-15, S5010 aero; batch_qp5.sh) | sourced |
| `stop` — mechanical stop | 20 | deg | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- share of the stop used: `use = peak / stop` = `11.5 / 20` = **0.575 **

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 0.575 vs 0.575.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **0.575 **, limit ≤ 0.6 , margin **+4.2%**
- Limit comes from: BOARD QP-4 s3: <= 0.60 of the 20 deg stop (G5 basis). At most 0.60 (QP-4 board basis).
- Passes. The result is 4.2 % under the limit (0.025  to spare).
- Status CHECKED: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- Verifies: A-P11 Cruise control authority

**6. How to check it in real life.** Log elevon commands during the first transition.

#### Servo torque factor at 7.2 V, 25 C (ES3054HV)
`CL-A-SERVO-FACTOR` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The elevon servo must be strong enough to hold the elevon against the airflow at top speed with a safety factor of 1.5 on top. This is the 25 C case.

**2. The formula.**

$$ F = \frac{M_{\mathrm{servo}}}{k\,\left(M_{\mathrm{hinge}} + M_{\mathrm{preload}} + M_{\mathrm{damper}}\right)} $$

In words: factor = servo torque / (load factor x (hinge moment + preload + damper at slew))

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `stall_72_nm` | servo stall torque at 7.2 V |
| `hinge_vd_nm` | aerodynamic hinge moment at VD |
| `preload_nm` | hinge friction preload |
| `damper_slew_nm` | damper drag while moving |
| `load_factor` | safety factor on load |
| `damper_slew_cold_nm` | damper drag when cold |
| `arm_mm` | servo arm length |
| `horn_mm` | elevon horn length |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `stall_72_nm` — servo stall torque at 7.2 V | 0.397 | N m | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `hinge_vd_nm` — aerodynamic hinge moment at VD | 0.151 | N m | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `preload_nm` — hinge friction preload | 0.0093 | N m | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `damper_slew_nm` — damper drag while moving | 0.0098 | N m | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | sourced |
| `damper_slew_cold_nm` — damper drag when cold | 0.0196 | N m | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed |
| `load_factor` — safety factor on load | 1.5 |  | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | sourced |
| `arm_mm` — servo arm length | 12 | mm | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | sourced |
| `horn_mm` — elevon horn length | 12.9 | mm | [QP-4 fixes 2 (FIXES2.md, supersedes FIXES where they differ)](../../../../../redesign/review/QP-4/FIXES2.md) (s1 flutter with damper, s2 servo, s3 gates, s6 foam, s7 mass and cost) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- torque factor: `factor_25c = stall_72_nm / (load_factor × (hinge_vd_nm + preload_nm + damper_slew_nm))` = `0.397 / (1.5 × (0.151 + 0.0093 + 0.0098))` = **1.5559 **

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 1.5559 vs 1.5559.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Limit comes from: FIXES2 s2: factor >= 1.5 on the required torque (1.5 x load). At least 1.5 (requirement A-P11).
- Passes. The result is 3.7 % above the limit (0.056  to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.

  - hinge_vd_nm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (aerodynamic hinge moment at VD)
  - preload_nm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (hinge friction preload)
  - damper_slew_cold_nm: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (damper drag when cold)
- Verifies: A-P11 Cruise control authority

## Flutter, divergence and gusts

Can the wing and elevons shake themselves apart at speed? Is there a safe gap to our top speed?

#### Flutter / divergence line, 1.2 x VD
`CL-A-FLUTTER-LINE` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** The speed every flutter, divergence and reversal result must beat: 1.2 times the design dive speed.

**2. The formula.**

$$ V_{\mathrm{line}} = 1.2\,V_D $$

In words: line = factor x VD

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `k` | safety factor 1.2 |
| `vd` | design dive speed |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `k` — safety factor 1.2 | 1.2 |  | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |
| `vd` — design dive speed | 24 | m/s | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- flutter line: `v_ms = k × vd` = `1.2 × 24` = **28.8 m/s**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 28.8 vs 28.8.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **28.8 m/s**
- No pass / fail limit: informational.
- Status CHECKED: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- Verifies: A-P13 Loads and flutter (G8)

**6. How to check it in real life.** Flight envelope: never exceed VD (24 m/s); an airspeed alarm at VD.

#### Elevon flutter onset with the hinge damper, nominal 25 C (lowest of kd / nom / hi)
`CL-A-FLUTTER-ELEVON-NOM` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** Elevon flutter is the shaking of the control surface on its hinge. At 25 C this is the lowest speed at which it begins, with the hinge damper fitted.

**2. The formula.**

`onset = lowest speed over the three corners`

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `x` | onset speed in each corner (kd, nominal, hi) |

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x` — onset speed in each corner (kd, nominal, hi) | table, 3 rows (below) | m/s | [Elevon flutter, lead 12.8 g (D-205; flutter_d205.md, else as flutter_d195.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/structures/flutter_d205.md) (elevon 6.7 g, lead 12.8 g at 20 mm (D-205), damper c 1.03e-3 hot / nominal / cold / half; p-method K sweep) | sourced |

Table `x`:

| c1 |
|---|
| 41 |
| 44 |
| 49 |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- lowest onset: `v_ms = min(x)` = `min([41, 44, 49])` = **41 m/s**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 41 vs 41.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **41 m/s**, limit ≥ 28.8 m/s, margin **+42.4%**
- Limit comes from: Flutter / divergence line, 1.2 x VD. At least 1.2 x VD = 28.8 m/s.
- Passes. The result is 42 % above the limit (12 m/s to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - assumption: Cap and damper drag and damper leak mitigations hold

## Vibration

Do the structure's natural frequencies stay away from the motor frequencies, and does the flight computer feel acceptable vibration?

#### Upper edge allowed for strongly coupled modes below the rotor bands
`CL-A-VIB-LOW-LIMIT` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** Structure modes below the rotor bands must be below this frequency, so they stay 20 % clear of the lowest spin frequency.

**2. The formula.**

$$ f_{\mathrm{low}} = \frac{n_{\min}/60}{1.2} $$

In words: limit = low 1P edge (rpm / 60) / 1.2

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `rpm` | lowest hover rpm |
| `sep` | required 20 % separation |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rpm` — lowest hover rpm | 6018 | rpm | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |
| `sep` — required 20 % separation | 1.2 |  | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- upper edge of the allowed low zone: `f_hz = rpm / 60 / sep` = `6018 / 60 / 1.2` = **83.583 Hz**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 83.583 vs 83.583.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **83.583 Hz**
- No pass / fail limit: informational.
- Status CHECKED: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- Verifies: A-P10 Vibration rule D-185

**6. How to check it in real life.** Tap test on the built cage (T-A-TAP).

#### Lower edge allowed for strongly coupled modes above the rotor bands
`CL-A-VIB-HIGH-LIMIT` · status **CHECKED**

**1. What is being checked.** Modes above the rotor bands must be above this frequency.

**2. The formula.**

$$ f_{\mathrm{high}} = 1.2\, f_{2P,\max} $$

In words: limit = high 2P edge x 1.2

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `f` | highest cruise 2P frequency |
| `sep` | required 20 % separation |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `f` — highest cruise 2P frequency | 253 | Hz | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |
| `sep` — required 20 % separation | 1.2 |  | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- lower edge of the allowed high zone: `f_hz = f × sep` = `253 × 1.2` = **303.6 Hz**

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 303.6 vs 303.6.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **303.6 Hz**
- No pass / fail limit: informational.
- Status CHECKED: The arithmetic works, the limit is met, and every input is traced to a repo file or vendor source that was compared on the date shown. Still no physical test.
- Verifies: A-P10 Vibration rule D-185

**6. How to check it in real life.** Tap test (T-A-TAP).

## Packaging and the 3-D model

Does it fit in a car, and does the CAD model assemble with no parts colliding?

#### Packed box against the 1.2 x 0.5 x 0.4 m car-trunk box
`CL-A-BOX-FIT` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The packed aircraft must fit in a car trunk. The test is the biggest ratio of packed size to the 1.2 x 0.5 x 0.4 m box limit.

**2. The formula.**

$$ \rho = \max_i \frac{a_i}{b_i} $$

In words: ratio = max over the three sorted dimensions of packed / limit

| Symbol | Means |
|---|---|
| `box` | packed dimensions |
| `lim` | box limit |

**3. The inputs.**

| Input | Value | Unit | Where it came from | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `box` — packed dimensions | table, 3 rows (below) | m | [Concept A requirements (REQUIREMENTS-A.md)](../../../../../redesign/qp/A/REQUIREMENTS-A.md) (limits of A-U1 ... A-D12; status PROPOSED, owner A2) | sourced |
| `lim` — box limit | table, 3 rows (below) | m | [Configuration-study transport requirement C3](../../../../../redesign/config-study/requirements/REQUIREMENTS.md) (C3(a): one box <= 1.2 x 0.5 x 0.4 m fits a mid-size sedan trunk (the team's car trunk is not measured)) | sourced |

Table `box`:

| c1 |
|---|
| 0.9 |
| 0.5 |
| 0.39 |

Table `lim`:

| c1 |
|---|
| 1.2 |
| 0.5 |
| 0.4 |

**4. The arithmetic.**

- worst ratio: `ratio = max(a / b for a, b in zip(sorted(box, reverse=True), sorted(lim, reverse=True)))` = **1 **

Recomputed here: matches the stored value 1 vs 1.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **1 **, limit ≤ 1 , margin **+0.00%**
- Limit comes from: fixed in the claim. At most 1.0 (fits).
- Passes. The result is 0 % under the limit (0  to spare).
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - assumption: The packed box fits the team's car trunk
- Verifies: A-U3 Packed aircraft fits one car trunk

**6. How to check it in real life.** Measure the real trunk and the packed aircraft with props on (the trunk size is not measured yet).

**4. The arithmetic.**

- The schedule is a day-by-day simulation (schedule_a.py logic) that a calculator cannot redo; run python -I redesign/qp/A/schedule_a.py to reproduce it.

**5. Result against the limit.**

- Result: **10 d**, limit ≥ 0 d, margin **+10**
- Limit comes from: fixed in the claim.
- The result is 10 against a limit of 0 and the margin is the distance to zero (+10); the sign decides pass or fail.
- Status UNVERIFIED: The arithmetic works and the limit is met, but at least one input is an unconfirmed estimate or an upstream claim is itself unverified. Treat as 'probably fine, not proven'.
  - gates: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (days per gate F01 to F06 (booked mornings))
  - repair: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (days for a crash repair loop)
  - redo: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (mornings to redo F04)
  - recess: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (spring recess start and end)
  - p: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (fraction of booked mornings that are flyable (estimate))
  - p_sens: ESTIMATE / UNVERIFIED: not yet measured or confirmed (pessimistic flyable fraction)
- Verifies: A-D8 First tethered hover by 2027-02-18

**6. How to check it in real life.** Compare the real gate dates against the plan every week; re-plan when the main order slips past 28 December.

#### Slack at p 0.60 (sensitivity, not the baseline)
`CL-A-SCHED-SENS` · status **UNVERIFIED**

**1. What is being checked.** The same slack if only 60 % of booked mornings are flyable instead of 72 % (a sensitivity, not the plan).

**2. The formula.**

`Gates F01 to F06 take (days per gate) at booked x p mornings a week, with one crash loop after F04. slack = deadline - end of F06.`

| `gates` | days per gate F01 to F06 (booked mornings) |

| `booked` | booked mornings a week |
